     1	// src/features/matters/useClientMap.ts
     2	import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react';
     3	import { useClientMapStore } from '@/platform/clientMap/clientMapStore';
     4	import { buildClientMap } from '@/features/matters/clientMap/generator';
     5	import { computeSourceFingerprint, proposeUpdates, mergePendingUpdates } from '@/platform/clientMap/updater';
     6	import type { ClientMap } from '@/platform/clientMap/types';
     7	import type { AuditEntry } from '@/platform/types/audit';
     8	import { isConfidentialityChoiceRequiredError } from '@/platform/privacy/localOnlyGuard';
     9	
    10	export type ClientMapStatus = 'idle' | 'generating' | 'ready' | 'empty' | 'error';
    11	
    12	/** Matters whose Client Map build is currently in flight. Shared across all hook
    13	 *  instances so the auto-build effect and the manual build can't fire two
    14	 *  concurrent builds for the same matter — a React StrictMode double-invoke (dev)
    15	 *  or a fast manual click otherwise spends duplicate AI cost + audit entries. */
    16	const clientMapBuildsInFlight = new Set<string>();
    17	
    18	/** A stored map is "ready" if it holds something to show: a section item, a
    19	 *  pending update, or an open gap question (which the Guided Interview works
    20	 *  through). A truly empty stored object surfaces the honest empty state
    21	 *  (BUG-103); a gap-only map is NOT empty, so the interview can render (Codex
    22	 *  finding 4). */
    23	function mapHasContent(map: ClientMap | undefined): boolean {
    24	  if (!map) return false;
    25	  return (
    26	    map.sections.some((s) => s.items.length > 0) ||
    27	    map.pendingUpdates.length > 0 ||
    28	    map.completeness.ask.length > 0 ||
    29	    map.sections.some((s) => s.kind === 'custom') // a user-added section is real content
    30	  );
    31	}
    32	
    33	/** Any state a regenerate must NOT blow away. Broader than mapHasContent: it
    34	 *  also covers recorded resolved gaps and prior dismissals, so an AI rebuild
    35	 *  never silently wipes the professional's decisions (BUG-101 airtight; Codex
    36	 *  finding 1). */
    37	function mapHasPreservableState(map: ClientMap | undefined): boolean {
    38	  if (!map) return false;
    39	  return (
    40	    mapHasContent(map) ||
    41	    (map.resolvedGaps?.length ?? 0) > 0 ||
    42	    (map.dismissedSignatures?.length ?? 0) > 0
    43	  );
    44	}
    45	
    46	export function useClientMap(
    47	  matterId: string,
    48	  options?: {
    49	    onAuditLog?: (entry: Omit<AuditEntry, 'id' | 'timestamp'>) => void;
    50	    /**
    51	     * When true, automatically build the map the first time a matter is opened
    52	     * with no map yet (status 'idle'), so a connector-created client — or any
    53	     * client — shows a populated, cited map with NO manual "Open Client Map"
    54	     * step. Mirrors the Matter-at-a-Glance auto-run on open. Cheap for
    55	     * content-less matters (buildClientMap short-circuits before any AI call)
    56	     * and runs at most once (the result is cached; status leaves 'idle' after
    57	     * the first build, and a build error lands on 'error', not back on 'idle',
    58	     * so it never retry-loops).
    59	     */
    60	    autoBuild?: boolean;
    61	  },
    62	): {
    63	  status: ClientMapStatus; errorMessage: string | null; map: ClientMap | undefined; generate: () => Promise<void>; checkForUpdates: () => Promise<void>;
    64	} {
    65	  const map = useClientMapStore((s) => s.maps[matterId]);
    66	  const setMap = useClientMapStore((s) => s.setMap);
    67	  const onAuditLog = options?.onAuditLog;
    68	  const [status, setStatus] = useState<ClientMapStatus>(mapHasContent(map) ? 'ready' : map ? 'empty' : 'idle');
    69	  const [errorMessage, setErrorMessage] = useState<string | null>(null);
    70	  const generate = useCallback(async () => {
    71	    // Dedupe concurrent builds for the same matter (auto-build vs manual click,
    72	    // StrictMode double-invoke). A build already in flight wins; this call no-ops.
    73	    if (clientMapBuildsInFlight.has(matterId)) return;
    74	    clientMapBuildsInFlight.add(matterId);
    75	    setStatus('generating');
    76	    setErrorMessage(null);
    77	    try {
    78	      // Fingerprint the source BEFORE the build, so the stored fingerprint
    79	      // reflects the source state the build actually saw. If content arrives
    80	      // DURING the build (e.g. a Wealthbox sync indexes this client mid-build),
    81	      // the post-build source differs from this fingerprint, so the next
    82	      // checkForUpdates detects the change and rebuilds — instead of the old
    83	      // order, which computed the fingerprint AFTER the build and could store the
    84	      // NEW fingerprint against an EMPTY map, leaving it permanently stuck empty
    85	      // (Codex race). Computing before only ever errs toward one extra rebuild,
    86	      // never toward a missed one.
    87	      const fp = await computeSourceFingerprint(matterId);
    88	      const built = await buildClientMap(
    89	        matterId,
    90	        onAuditLog ? { onAuditLog } : undefined,
    91	      );
    92	
    93	      const existing = useClientMapStore.getState().getMap(matterId);
    94	
    95	      if (mapHasPreservableState(existing) && existing !== undefined) {
    96	        // A map already exists with the professional's own work (user-origin
    97	        // items, accepted updates, custom sections, resolved gaps, dismissals).
    98	        // NEVER blindly overwrite it. Route fresh AI content through the
    99	        // approve-first tray instead, and keep the existing map intact.
   100	        const dismissed = existing.dismissedSignatures ?? [];
   101	        const proposals = proposeUpdates(matterId, existing, built, dismissed, fp);
   102	        const nextMap = {
   103	          ...existing,
   104	          pendingUpdates: mergePendingUpdates(existing.pendingUpdates, proposals, dismissed),
   105	          lastSourceFingerprint: fp,
   106	        };
   107	        useClientMapStore.getState().setMap(matterId, nextMap);
   108	        // Clear the transient 'generating'; the returned status is re-derived
   109	        // from the stored map's actual content below.
   110	        setStatus(mapHasContent(nextMap) ? 'ready' : 'empty');
   111	        return;
   112	      }
   113	
   114	      // First build (or a previously-empty map): full store is safe.
   115	      const builtMap = { ...built, lastSourceFingerprint: fp };
   116	      setMap(matterId, builtMap);
   117	      setStatus(mapHasContent(builtMap) ? 'ready' : 'empty');
   118	    } catch (error) {
   119	      console.error('Client Map build failed', error);
   120	      setErrorMessage(
   121	        isConfidentialityChoiceRequiredError(error)
   122	          ? error.message
   123	          : 'Could not build client map. Check your AI connection and try again.',
   124	      );
   125	      setStatus('error');
   126	    } finally {
   127	      clientMapBuildsInFlight.delete(matterId);
   128	    }
   129	  }, [matterId, setMap, onAuditLog]);
   130	
   131	  const checkForUpdates = useCallback(async () => {
   132	    let claimedBuild = false;
   133	    try {
   134	      const before = useClientMapStore.getState().getMap(matterId);
   135	      if (!before || before.lastBuiltAt === '') return; // nothing built yet — cheap out
   136	      const fp = await computeSourceFingerprint(matterId);
   137	      // Close the duplicate-build TOCTOU (Codex #4). These checks run SYNCHRONOUSLY
   138	      // after the async fingerprint and before the build, so nothing interleaves
   139	      // between them:
   140	      //   (a) if a build is already in flight, no-op — it covers this recovery
   141	      //       (catches the OVERLAP where the other call is mid-build and has not
   142	      //       stored its fingerprint yet);
   143	      //   (b) RE-READ the latest stored map and compare fp against ITS fingerprint,
   144	      //       NOT the stale pre-await snapshot — catches the harder ordering where a
   145	      //       concurrent check fully FINISHED (built, stored this exact fp, cleared
   146	      //       the guard) WHILE we were still fingerprinting. Skip if unchanged.
   147	      if (clientMapBuildsInFlight.has(matterId)) return;
   148	      const current = useClientMapStore.getState().getMap(matterId);
   149	      if (!current || current.lastBuiltAt === '' || fp === current.lastSourceFingerprint) return;
   150	      clientMapBuildsInFlight.add(matterId);
   151	      claimedBuild = true;
   152	      const fresh = await buildClientMap(
   153	        matterId,
   154	        onAuditLog ? { onAuditLog } : undefined,
   155	      );
   156	      // Re-read the latest stored map AFTER the async build — it may have gained
   157	      // user edits, accepted updates, or pending proposals while we were building.
   158	      const latest = useClientMapStore.getState().getMap(matterId) ?? current;
   159	      const dismissed = latest.dismissedSignatures ?? [];
   160	      const proposals = proposeUpdates(matterId, latest, fresh, dismissed);
   161	      // NEVER replace items, and NEVER silently drop a still-pending proposal the
   162	      // user hasn't reviewed (BUG-104): merge the fresh proposals into the existing
   163	      // tray by stable signature. Keep current sections; only update the fingerprint.
   164	      useClientMapStore.getState().setMap(matterId, {
   165	        ...latest,
   166	        pendingUpdates: mergePendingUpdates(latest.pendingUpdates, proposals, dismissed),
   167	        lastSourceFingerprint: fp,
   168	      });
   169	    } catch (error) {
   170	      console.error('Client Map update check failed', error);
   171	      setErrorMessage(
   172	        isConfidentialityChoiceRequiredError(error)
   173	          ? error.message
   174	          : 'Could not check for client map updates. Try again in a moment.',
   175	      );
   176	      setStatus('error');
   177	    } finally {
   178	      if (claimedBuild) {
   179	        clientMapBuildsInFlight.delete(matterId);
   180	      }
   181	    }
   182	  }, [matterId, onAuditLog]);
   183	
   184	  // Transient/terminal states from an in-flight or failed generate win; otherwise
   185	  // derive from the stored map's actual content so an empty matter shows the
   186	  // honest empty state instead of a blank "ready" map (BUG-103).
   187	  const resolvedStatus: ClientMapStatus =
   188	    status === 'generating' || status === 'error'
   189	      ? status
   190	      : mapHasContent(map)
   191	        ? 'ready'
   192	        : map
   193	          ? 'empty'
   194	          : status;
   195	
   196	  // Auto-build on first open (opt-in). 'idle' means no map is stored yet, so this
   197	  // fires once per matter the first time it is viewed, then never again (the
   198	  // build moves status off 'idle' and the result is cached). See the autoBuild
   199	  // option doc above for why this is safe + cheap.
   200	  const autoBuild = options?.autoBuild ?? false;
   201	  useEffect(() => {
   202	    if (autoBuild && resolvedStatus === 'idle') {
   203	      // Defer out of the synchronous effect body so generate()'s initial
   204	      // setStatus('generating') doesn't run synchronously inside the effect
   205	      // (avoids cascading renders — same pattern the at-a-glance auto-run uses).
   206	      void Promise.resolve().then(() => { void generate(); });
   207	    }
   208	  }, [autoBuild, resolvedStatus, generate]);
   209	
   210	  return { status: resolvedStatus, errorMessage, map, generate, checkForUpdates };
   211	}

     2	import { MemoryService, isMemoryEnabled } from '@/platform/rag/MemoryService';
     3	import { buildWorkspaceContextBlock } from '@/platform/rag/workspaceCommand';

    18	
    19	// Gap questions are tagged with the section their answer belongs to so the
    20	// Guided Interview can file the answer in the right section. Unknown / missing
    21	// section names fall back to 'money'.
    22	const VALID_GAP_SECTIONS = new Set<string>(CORE_SECTION_ORDER);
    23	const DEFAULT_GAP_SECTION = 'money';
    24	const SECTION_NAME_LIST = CORE_SECTION_ORDER.join(', ');
    25	
    26	/** Parse the gap-questions AI response. Accepts both the section-tagged shape
    27	 *  ({text, section}) and a plain string list (back-compat); both are coerced to
    28	 *  GapQuestion with a validated sectionKey. */
    29	export function parseGapQuestions(content: string): GapQuestion[] {
    30	  let raw = content.trim();
    31	  if (raw.startsWith('```')) raw = raw.replace(/^```(?:json)?\s*/i, '').replace(/\s*```$/, '').trim();
    32	  try {
    33	    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { questions?: unknown };
    34	    if (!Array.isArray(parsed.questions)) return [];
    35	    const out: GapQuestion[] = [];
    36	    for (const q of parsed.questions.slice(0, 5)) {
    37	      if (typeof q === 'string') {
    38	        const text = q.trim();
    39	        if (text) out.push({ text, sectionKey: DEFAULT_GAP_SECTION });
    40	        continue;
    41	      }
    42	      if (q && typeof q === 'object' && typeof (q as { text?: unknown }).text === 'string') {
    43	        const text = (q as { text: string }).text.trim();
    44	        if (!text) continue;
    45	        const section = (q as { section?: unknown }).section;
    46	        const sectionKey = typeof section === 'string' && VALID_GAP_SECTIONS.has(section) ? section : DEFAULT_GAP_SECTION;
    47	        out.push({ text, sectionKey });
    48	      }
    49	    }
    50	    return out;
    51	  } catch {
    52	    return [];
    53	  }
    54	}
    55	
    56	const SECTION_QUERIES: Record<CoreSectionKey, string> = {
    57	  household: 'household members spouse children dependents beneficiaries key contacts CPA estate attorney prior advisor life situation job change who the client is',
    58	  goals: 'goals priorities objectives what they want retirement timeline values concerns risk tolerance risk profile',
    59	  money: 'accounts assets liabilities net worth holdings balances custodian Schwab portfolio insurance estate documents beneficiary designations mortgage cash reserve time horizon',
    60	  followups: 'prior advice recommendations decisions next steps follow up open items promises to do tasks rollover Roth conversion rebalancing upcoming reviews meetings deadlines key dates required minimum distributions',

    62	const ASK_QUERY = 'what key facts are still unknown or unclear about this client';
    63	const TOP_K = 8;
    64	
    65	const sectionPrompt = (title: string, ctx: string) =>
    66	  `You are a private assistant building a client/household profile section: "${title}".
    67	${ctx}
    68	Return ONLY JSON (no fences): {"items":[{"text":"one short factual sentence","sourceNumbers":[1],"assumption":false}]}.
    69	Rules: base every item ONLY on the context; cite the [N] numbers that support it in sourceNumbers; if you must infer without a source, set assumption true and sourceNumbers []; under 20 words each; no em dashes; empty items array if nothing applies.`;

    71	export async function buildClientMap(
    72	  matterId: string,
    73	  options?: {
    74	    signal?: AbortSignal;
    75	    onAuditLog?: (entry: Omit<AuditEntry, 'id' | 'timestamp'>) => void;
    76	  },
    77	): Promise<ClientMap> {
    78	  if (!isMemoryEnabled()) return { ...emptyClientMap(matterId), lastBuiltAt: new Date().toISOString() };
    79	  const scope = { kind: 'matter' as const, matterId };
    80	
    81	  // Retrieve per section first; if everything is empty, short-circuit.
    82	  // Connector-access: filter recognized RightCapital/Jump exports at the
    83	  // retrieval source when consent has not been given, so the prompt context, the
    84	  // generated items' citations, and the "any content?" decision all use the same
    85	  // consented set (no withheld export surfaces as a cited source).
    86	  const perSection = await Promise.all(
    87	    CORE_SECTION_ORDER.map(async (key) => ({ key, hits: filterHitsForExportConsent(await MemoryService.retrieve(SECTION_QUERIES[key], TOP_K, scope, false)) })),
    88	  );
    89	  const askHits = filterHitsForExportConsent(await MemoryService.retrieve(ASK_QUERY, TOP_K, scope, false));
    90	  const anyContent = perSection.some((p) => p.hits.length > 0) || askHits.length > 0;
    91	  const totalHits = perSection.reduce((sum, p) => sum + p.hits.length, 0) + askHits.length;
    92	  const topScore = [...perSection.flatMap((p) => p.hits), ...askHits].reduce<number | null>(
    93	    (max, hit) => (max === null ? hit.score : Math.max(max, hit.score)),
    94	    null,
    95	  );
    96	  options?.onAuditLog?.(auditEventToEntry({
    97	    type: 'retrieval_executed',
    98	    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
    99	    payload: {
   100	      query: 'Client Map build',
   101	      scope,
   102	      hitCount: totalHits,
   103	      topScore,
   104	    },
   105	  }));
   106	  if (!anyContent || options?.signal?.aborted) {
   107	    return { ...emptyClientMap(matterId), lastBuiltAt: new Date().toISOString() };
   108	  }
   109	
   110	  const resolvedProvider = await buildResolvedProviderForClientMap();
   111	  const provider = resolvedProvider.provider;
   112	  const emitAiAudit = (
   113	    label: string,
   114	    response: { content: string; usage?: { inputTokens?: number; outputTokens?: number }; cost?: number },
   115	  ) => {
   116	    const egress = resolveEgress({
   117	      provider: resolvedProvider.providerId,
   118	      mode: getConfidentialityMode(),
   119	      isDemo: false,
   120	      assuredAvailable: false,
   121	    });
   122	    options?.onAuditLog?.(auditEventToEntry({
   123	      type: 'egress',
   124	      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
   125	      payload: {
   126	        provider: egress.provider,
   127	        model: resolvedProvider.model,
   128	        mode: getConfidentialityMode(),
   129	        destination: egress.destination,
   130	        dataLeaves: egress.dataLeaves,
   131	        scope,
   132	      },
   133	    }));
   134	    options?.onAuditLog?.({
   135	      action: 'model_call',
   136	      description: `Client Map ${label} to ${resolvedProvider.model}`,
   137	      model: resolvedProvider.model,
   138	      inputs: { matterId, step: label },
   139	      outputs: { contentLength: response.content.length },
   140	      userDecision: 'auto',
   141	      metadata: { feature: 'client_map', step: label },
   142	      tokensIn: response.usage?.inputTokens ?? 0,
   143	      tokensOut: response.usage?.outputTokens ?? 0,
   144	      costUsd: response.cost ?? 0,
   145	      provider: resolvedProvider.providerId,
   146	    });
   147	  };
   148	  const sections: ClientMapSection[] = [];
   149	  for (const { key, hits } of perSection) {
   150	    if (hits.length === 0) { sections.push({ id: key, kind: 'core', key, title: CORE_SECTION_TITLE[key], items: [] }); continue; }
   151	    const ctx = buildWorkspaceContextBlock(hits);
   152	    // Race guard (Ask's gold pattern): re-check the CURRENT mode AFTER all awaits,
   153	    // immediately before each send — a flip to Local-only mid-build must never
   154	    // send this client's context to the cloud. Re-checked per section because the
   155	    // prior section's await is itself a window for the mode to change.
   156	    assertLocalOnlyAllowsSend(resolvedProvider.providerId);
   157	    const res = await provider.sendMessage('Build this section.', { systemPrompt: sectionPrompt(CORE_SECTION_TITLE[key], ctx), maxTokens: 500 });
   158	    emitAiAudit(key, res);
   159	    sections.push({
   160	      id: key,
   161	      kind: 'core',
   162	      key,
   163	      title: CORE_SECTION_TITLE[key],
   164	      items: capGeneratedSectionItems(itemsFromRaw(parseItems(res.content), hits)),
   165	    });
   166	  }
   167	
   168	  // Gap questions for Context Completeness. Each gap carries the section its
   169	  // answer belongs to, so the Guided Interview files the answer in the right place.
   170	  let ask: GapQuestion[] = [];
   171	  if (askHits.length > 0) {
   172	    const ctx = buildWorkspaceContextBlock(askHits);
   173	    // Same race guard immediately before the gap-questions send.
   174	    assertLocalOnlyAllowsSend(resolvedProvider.providerId);
   175	    const res = await provider.sendMessage('List the gap questions.', {
   176	      systemPrompt: `Given this client context, list up to 5 short questions whose answers are missing and that you would need to ask the client. For each question name the section its answer belongs to: one of ${SECTION_NAME_LIST}. ${ctx} Return ONLY JSON (no fences): {"questions":[{"text":"...","section":"money"}]}. No em dashes.`,
   177	      maxTokens: 400,
   178	    });
   179	    emitAiAudit('gap_questions', res);
   180	    ask = parseGapQuestions(res.content);
   181	  }
   182	
   183	  // B6: collapse facts that surfaced in more than one section (e.g. the same fact
   184	  // from a file source AND a CRM source on a merged client) so it shows once.
   185	  const dedupedSections = dedupeAcrossSections(sections);
   186	
   187	  return {
   188	    matterId,
   189	    sections: dedupedSections,
   190	    completeness: deriveCompleteness(dedupedSections, ask),
   191	    pendingUpdates: [],
   192	    lastBuiltAt: new Date().toISOString(),
   193	    lastSourceFingerprint: '',
   194	  };
   195	}

    12	  'a', 'about', 'after', 'again', 'all', 'also', 'an', 'and', 'any', 'are', 'as', 'at',
    13	  'be', 'been', 'being', 'but', 'by', 'client', 'clients', 'did', 'do', 'does', 'for',

    21	  expectations: 'expect',
    22	  expected: 'expect',
    23	  expects: 'expect',
    24	  holding: 'hold',

    39	
    40	/**
    41	 * Cheap staleness fingerprint of a matter's indexed content.
    42	 *
    43	 * BUG-102: it folds in each hit's content-addressed chunk `id` (which DOES
    44	 * change when a file is edited in place and re-indexed), not just the set of
    45	 * unique source paths. That way an in-place edit of an existing file (same path,
    46	 * new content -> new chunk id) changes the fingerprint and triggers an update
    47	 * pass, instead of being invisible because the path set is unchanged.
    48	 */
    49	export async function computeSourceFingerprint(matterId: string): Promise<string> {
    50	  const hits = await MemoryService.retrieve(BROAD_QUERY, 200, { kind: 'matter', matterId }, false);
    51	  const sources = Array.from(new Set(hits.map((h) => h.sourceId ?? h.path))).sort();
    52	  // Content-addressed chunk ids change on every in-place edit; include them so
    53	  // an edited-but-same-path file is detected. Fall back to a path:paragraph
    54	  // locator for any pre-id row so those still contribute some content signal.
    55	  const chunkKeys = Array.from(
    56	    new Set(hits.map((h) => h.id ?? `${h.sourceId ?? h.path}#${String(h.paragraphIndex)}`)),
    57	  ).sort();
    58	  return `${String(sources.length)}:${sources.join('|')}::${String(chunkKeys.length)}:${chunkKeys.join('|')}`;
    59	}
    60	
    61	/** Normalize an item's text for stable comparison/signature. */
    62	function normalizeText(text: string): string {
    63	  return text.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, ' ');
    64	}
    65	
    66	function normalizeToken(token: string): string {
    67	  const alias = TOKEN_ALIASES[token];
    68	  if (alias) return alias;
    69	  if (token.length > 5 && token.endsWith('ies')) return `${token.slice(0, -3)}y`;
    70	  if (token.length > 5 && token.endsWith('ing')) return token.slice(0, -3);
    71	  if (token.length > 4 && token.endsWith('ed')) return token.slice(0, -2);
    72	  if (token.length > 4 && token.endsWith('es')) return token.slice(0, -2);
    73	  if (token.length > 3 && token.endsWith('s')) return token.slice(0, -1);
    74	  return token;
    75	}
    76	
    77	function contentWordSet(text: string): Set<string> {
    78	  const tokens = normalizeText(text)
    79	    .replace(/['’]s\b/g, '')
    80	    .match(/[a-z0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)?/g) ?? [];
    81	  return new Set(
    82	    tokens
    83	      .map(normalizeToken)
    84	      .filter((token) => token.length > 1 && !LOW_SIGNAL_WORDS.has(token)),
    85	  );
    86	}
    87	
    88	export function areNearDuplicateFacts(left: string, right: string): boolean {
    89	  if (normalizeText(left) === normalizeText(right)) return true;
    90	
    91	  const leftTokens = contentWordSet(left);
    92	  const rightTokens = contentWordSet(right);
    93	  const smallerSize = Math.min(leftTokens.size, rightTokens.size);
    94	  if (smallerSize < MIN_NEAR_DUPLICATE_TOKENS) return false;
    95	
    96	  let shared = 0;
    97	  for (const token of leftTokens) {
    98	    if (rightTokens.has(token)) shared += 1;
    99	  }
   100	  const unionSize = leftTokens.size + rightTokens.size - shared;

     1	/**
     2	 * MemoryService — opt-out wrapper around the Tauri RAG commands.
     3	 *
     4	 * The Settings panel has a `memory.enabled` toggle (defaults to `true`). When
     5	 * disabled, every entry point here short-circuits without invoking the
     6	 * embedder or LanceDB:
     7	 *
     8	 *   - `indexFile` / `indexWorkspace`: resolve immediately
     9	 *   - `retrieve`: returns `[]`
    10	 *
    11	 * That means ALL future memory features (M1 retrieval, M2 @workspace,
    12	 * M3 facts) can be disabled with a single toggle without each call site
    13	 * having to know about the setting. Importantly the toggle controls the
    14	 * RUNTIME behaviour only — there is no persistent disabling that orphans
    15	 * data in `<workspace>/.keepance/vectors/`. Re-enabling the toggle
    16	 * re-uses whatever has already been indexed.
    17	 */

    38	/**

    41	 * Tauri backend — `ragRetrieve` throws "RAG is only available in the desktop

    44	 * dependency-injection seam already used for the matter/privilege resolvers and

    83	/** How the toggle is read. Pluggable so tests can pass a stub. */

   371	  /** Retrieve from the local RAG store.
   372	   *
   373	   *  WS-B/C: `scope` is the confidentiality boundary and is REQUIRED (F2.6b — no
   374	   *  default). Every caller must state its scope: pass `{ kind: 'matter', matterId }`
   375	   *  for a per-client search, or `{ kind: 'allMatters' }` for a deliberate

   378	   *  clients by simply omitting the scope.
   379	   *
   380	   *  WS-PRIV: `includePrivileged` defaults to `false` — attorney-client and

    70	/// widen it; it cannot weaken isolation. Today only the contradiction finder
    71	/// passes a cap (perSourceCap: 4); chat retrieval is unchanged.
    72	/// WS3d-A — `enable_reranker` (camelCase `enableReranker` over IPC) is the
    73	/// OPTIONAL cross-encoder reranking layer. DEFAULT OFF: absent or `false`
    74	/// leaves retrieval byte-for-byte the vector-only path (same fetch size, same
    75	/// scores, same order). Only an explicit `true` turns it on, and even then it
    76	/// re-orders ONLY within the already-scoped, already-prefiltered candidate set
    77	/// `nearest()` returned — it can never widen scope, re-admit a prefiltered-out
    78	/// chunk, or change which matters/privilege are visible (re-ranking ≠
    79	/// re-retrieval). If the reranker model isn't downloaded or fails to load,
    80	/// retrieval transparently falls back to the vector-only order — never crashes,
    81	/// never blocks an answer.
    82	#[tauri::command]
    83	pub async fn rag_retrieve(
    84	    state: State<'_, RagState>,
    85	    query: String,
    86	    top_k: u32,
    87	    scope: RetrievalScope,
    88	    include_privileged: Option<bool>,
    89	    per_source_cap: Option<u32>,
    90	    enable_reranker: Option<bool>,
    91	    enable_hybrid_search: Option<bool>,
    92	) -> Result<Vec<Hit>, String> {
    93	    if query.trim().is_empty() || top_k == 0 {
    94	        return Ok(Vec::new());
    95	    }
    96	    // BUG-099 fail-closed: if the durable tombstone file was unreadable on
    97	    // workspace open, the real unsafe-token set is unknown, so we cannot prove a
    98	    // result is not stale. Refuse to serve (typed error the frontend surfaces as
    99	    // "memory needs rebuilding") rather than risk citing a stale row. Cleared by
   100	    // a clean full re-index, which rewrites the tombstone file.
   101	    if state.index_integrity_unknown.load(Ordering::SeqCst) {
   102	        return Err(
   103	            "memory integrity is uncertain (the safety record could not be read); \
   104	             re-index this workspace before searching"
   105	                .to_string(),
   106	        );
   107	    }
   108	    // WS-PRIV: absent (legacy callers) or false → EXCLUDE privileged content.
   109	    // Only an explicit `true` flips it. The default is the safe one.
   110	    let include_privileged = include_privileged.unwrap_or(false);
   111	    // F-510: cap == 0 (or absent) = no cap. With a cap we OVERFETCH so the
   112	    // per-source filter has candidates from other sources to fill from.
   113	    let cap = per_source_cap.unwrap_or(0) as usize;
   114	    // WS3d-A: default OFF. When ON, we likewise OVERFETCH so the cross-encoder
   115	    // can promote a strongly-relevant chunk the vector pass ranked just outside
   116	    // top_k into the final set, then we truncate back to top_k after re-sort.
   117	    // Every overfetched candidate still went through the SAME matter/privilege/
   118	    // tombstone prefilter inside nearest() — overfetch widens depth, never scope.
   119	    //
   120	    // EXACT fallback: we only overfetch when the reranker is actually going to
   121	    // run — i.e. the flag is on AND the model is present and loads
   122	    // (`ensure_ready`). If it's absent or fails to load, `want_rerank` is false
   123	    // and `fetch_k` stays `top_k`, so retrieval is byte-for-byte the vector-only
   124	    // path — never a top-K skimmed off a larger ANN query whose candidate
   125	    // exploration / tie order could differ. (Codex review, P2.)
   126	    let enable_reranker = enable_reranker.unwrap_or(false);
   127	    let want_rerank = enable_reranker && reranker::ensure_ready().await;
   128	    // WS3d-B: hybrid (BM25 keyword + vector) search, default OFF. When ON we
   129	    // overfetch the vector side too, so the fusion has depth to promote a keyword
   130	    // hit the vector pass ranked just outside top_k. OFF (the shipped default) is
   131	    // a TRUE no-op below: no keyword index touched, byte-for-byte the vector path.
   132	    let enable_hybrid = enable_hybrid_search.unwrap_or(false);
   133	    let fetch_k = if cap > 0 || want_rerank || enable_hybrid {
   134	        (top_k as usize).saturating_mul(4).min(200)
   135	    } else {
   136	        top_k as usize
   137	    };
   138	    // Resolve + validate the scope into the store-level filter argument BEFORE
   139	    // any work. Matter ids are validated here (defence-in-depth before the SQL
   140	    // prefilter). AllMatters → None (no filter; deliberate cross-matter).
   141	    let scope_filter: Option<String> = match &scope {
   142	        RetrievalScope::Matter { matter_id } => Some(
   143	            store::validate_matter_id(matter_id)
   144	                .map(|s| s.to_string())
   145	                .map_err(|e| format!("invalid scope matter_id: {e}"))?,

     1	// Matter / Client model (WS-B/C app)
     2	//
     3	// A "matter" is the confidentiality boundary in Advisor Prep Hero 3.0. It groups one
     4	// client's work under one or more workspace folders. Every file or email
     5	// indexed under a matter is tagged with that matter's id; retrieval is then
     6	// scoped to a single matter so one client's data can never surface inside a
     7	// chat about another client's matter.
     8	//
     9	// The simplest real-world model: a matter owns one or more top-level (or
    10	// nested) workspace folders. Any file whose path lives under one of those
    11	// folders belongs to the matter. Files outside every mapped folder fall back
    12	// to the `unassigned` sentinel — the same sentinel the Rust indexer uses when
    13	// no matterId is supplied (see `rag_index_*` in src-tauri/src/commands/rag).
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * The sentinel id used for any chunk that hasn't been mapped to a real
    17	 * matter. Mirrors the Rust-side `"unassigned"` default so the frontend and
    18	 * backend agree on the bucket an unmapped file lands in. Never persist a
    19	 * matter with this id; it is reserved.
    20	 */
    21	export const UNASSIGNED_MATTER_ID = 'unassigned';
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * A single client matter. `folderPaths` are ABSOLUTE workspace paths (the
    25	 * same shape the editor and RAG use), each the root of a folder whose files
    26	 * belong to this matter. A matter may map to several folders (e.g. a client
    27	 * with separate "Litigation" and "Corporate" trees).
    28	 */
    29	export interface Matter {
    30	  /** Stable unique id (uuid-ish). The citation/scope key the backend stores. */
    31	  id: string;
    32	  /** Human-readable matter name, e.g. "Acme v. Beta - Patent". */
    33	  name: string;
    34	  /** The client this matter belongs to, e.g. "Acme Corp". */
    35	  client: string;
    36	  /** Absolute folder paths whose files belong to this matter. */
    37	  folderPaths: string[];
    38	  /**
    39	   * Mail-folder keys whose email belongs to this matter. Each key is a mail
    40	   * folder identifier in the form `provider/account/folderId`, or
    41	   * `provider/account` for an account-level mapping (every folder in that
    42	   * account). Email synced from a mapped folder is indexed under this matter;
    43	   * see `mailFolderKey` / `parseMailFolderKey` in `matterResolver`.
    44	   *
    45	   * Optional so matters created before mail mapping landed still parse from
    46	   * persisted storage (a missing value is treated as an empty list).
    47	   */
    48	  mailFolderPaths?: string[];
    49	  /**
    50	   * Wealthbox household IDs whose CRM records belong to this matter. Each
    51	   * entry is the Wealthbox household id string returned by `crm_list_households`
    52	   * (and stored in `CrmHouseholdDto.id`). CRM objects synced for a matched
    53	   * household are indexed under this matter's scope.
    54	   *
    55	   * Optional so matters created before the Wealthbox connector landed still
    56	   * parse from persisted storage (a missing value is treated as an empty list).
    57	   */
    58	  crmHouseholdKeys?: string[];
    59	  /**
    60	   * OneDrive / SharePoint folder ids whose files belong to this matter.
    61	   * Per-connector mapping rules are added by the connector module; this shared
    62	   * slot lets the matter store persist those links without another schema edit.
    63	   */
    64	  onedriveFolderKeys?: string[];
    65	  /**
    66	   * Box folder ids whose files belong to this matter.
    67	   */
    68	  boxFolderKeys?: string[];
    69	  /**
    70	   * E-signature record keys whose envelopes / agreements belong to this matter.
    71	   * The first implementation targets DocuSign, but the persisted field is
    72	   * connector-neutral.
    73	   */
    74	  esignKeys?: string[];
    75	  /**
    76	   * Jotform form/submission keys whose records belong to this matter.
    77	   */
    78	  jotformKeys?: string[];
    79	  /**
    80	   * ShareFile folder ids whose files belong to this matter.
    81	   */
    82	  sharefileFolderKeys?: string[];
    83	  /**
    84	   * Meeting record keys whose scheduled events belong to this matter. The first
    85	   * implementation targets Calendly, but the persisted field is connector-neutral.
    86	   */
    87	  meetingKeys?: string[];
    88	  /**
    89	   * Zocks meeting/intelligence keys whose records belong to this matter.
    90	   */
    91	  zocksKeys?: string[];
    92	  /**
    93	   * Addepar household/account keys whose records belong to this matter.
    94	   */
    95	  addeparKeys?: string[];
    96	  /**
    97	   * True when this matter's DISPLAY identity (name/client) originated from a CRM
    98	   * connector (e.g. a Wealthbox household), as opposed to user-entered or
    99	   * file-derived. Used at disconnect time to scrub Wealthbox-derived names: a
   100	   * pure-CRM matter is deleted, but a CRM-created matter the user has since added
   101	   * files/mail to keeps its content while its imported name/client are scrubbed.
   102	   * Optional/absent for non-CRM and pre-flag matters (treated as `false`).
   103	   */
   104	  createdFromCrm?: boolean;
   105	  /**
   106	   * Privileged-matter designation. When `true`, this matter holds
   107	   * attorney-client / work-product material and is treated as a confidentiality
   108	   * boundary that must never be exfiltrated through a network-capable extension.
   109	   * When this matter is the ACTIVE matter, Privileged Matter Mode is forced on
   110	   * (network plugins + MCP are disabled). Optional so matters created before
   111	   * this flag landed parse cleanly (a missing value is treated as `false`).
   112	   */
   113	  privileged?: boolean;
   114	  /**
   115	   * Explicit opt-in for external AI tools that connect through Advisor Prep Hero's MCP
   116	   * server. Default is false. This is intentionally separate from the active
   117	   * matter: focusing a matter inside Advisor Prep Hero must never grant an outside AI
   118	   * client access to it.
   119	   */
   120	  mcpAccessGranted?: boolean;
   121	  /** ISO timestamp the matter was created. */
   122	  createdAt: string;
   123	
   124	  // ── Firm linkage (Phase 1, Task 3) ────────────────────────────────────────
   125	  // These fields are optional so matters created before firm wiring still parse
   126	  // cleanly. A matter is "shared" when `shared === true && firmMatterId` is set.
   127	  // Runtime sync status is NOT stored here; it lives in matterSyncStore.
   128	
   129	  /**
   130	   * The matter ID on the firm backend (UUID from POST /org/matters).

   301	
   302	/// Filename (under the vectors dir) holding the integer `INDEX_VERSION` the
   303	/// current `chunks` table was built with.
   304	const INDEX_VERSION_FILE: &str = ".index_version";
   305	
   306	/// SQL-escape a string for safe embedding inside an `only_if` predicate. Doubles
   307	/// single quotes (the only metacharacter that can break out of a single-quoted
   308	/// SQL string literal), identical to what `delete_path` already does. Matter
   309	/// ids are app-controlled, but the filter string is SECURITY-SENSITIVE so we
   310	/// always escape — a crafted matter_id must never be able to break the predicate.
   311	pub fn sql_escape(s: &str) -> String {
   312	    s.replace('\'', "''")
   313	}
   314	
   315	/// Validate a matter id before it is interpolated into a SQL `only_if` filter.
   316	/// Defence-in-depth on top of `sql_escape`: reject obviously malformed ids
   317	/// (empty, or containing control characters / NULs) so a scoped query can never
   318	/// be built from a degenerate scope key. Returns the id unchanged if valid.
   319	pub fn validate_matter_id(matter_id: &str) -> Result<&str> {
   320	    if matter_id.is_empty() {
   321	        anyhow::bail!(
   322	            "matter_id must not be empty (use UNASSIGNED_MATTER for uncategorized content)"
   323	        );
   324	    }
   325	    if matter_id.chars().any(|c| c == '\0' || c.is_control()) {
   326	        anyhow::bail!("matter_id contains control characters");
   327	    }
   328	    Ok(matter_id)
   329	}

     2	/**
     3	 * Calendar connector card (Account -> Connections). One screen, three paths:

    10	import { useState, useEffect } from 'react';

    36	// Durable, append-only audit trail for connector activity, so a calendar
    37	// sync (including one that indexed zero meetings or failed) always leaves a
    38	// record — the same honesty guarantee the Calendly/OneDrive paths give.
    39	const calendarAudit = new AuditService('connectors');
    40	
    41	const PROVIDER_LABEL: Record<CalendarProviderId, string> = {
    42	  outlook: 'Outlook',
    43	  google: 'Google',
    44	  ics: 'Calendar (.ics)',
    45	};
    46	
    47	// A pending OAuth sign-in can be aborted mid-flight (browser tab closed,
    48	// user backs out). Outlook and Google each have their own command, but both
    49	// set the same CalendarState.oauth_cancel flag on the Rust side — fine
    50	// because `busy` below only ever lets one OAuth flow be in flight at a time
    51	// from this card, and each connect attempt resets the flag to false first.
    52	const OAUTH_CANCEL: Record<'outlook' | 'google', () => Promise<void>> = {
    53	  outlook: calendarConnectOutlookCancel,
    54	  google: calendarConnectGoogleCancel,
    55	};
    56	
    57	export function CalendarConnect() {
    58	  const [connected, setConnected] = useState<
    59	    Record<CalendarProviderId, boolean>
    60	  >({
    61	    outlook: false,
    62	    google: false,
    63	    ics: false,
    64	  });
    65	  const [busy, setBusy] = useState<CalendarProviderId | null>(null);
    66	  const [syncing, setSyncing] = useState(false);
    67	  const [icsUrl, setIcsUrl] = useState('');
    68	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    69	  const [lastReport, setLastReport] = useState<CalendarSyncReport | null>(null);
    70	  const [lastSyncedAt, setLastSyncedAt] = useState<Date | null>(null);
    71	  const { confirm, dialogProps } = useConfirmDialog();
    72	
    73	  async function refreshConnected() {
    74	    const next: Record<CalendarProviderId, boolean> = {
    75	      outlook: false,
    76	      google: false,
    77	      ics: false,
    78	    };
    79	    for (const id of Object.keys(PROVIDER_LABEL) as CalendarProviderId[]) {
    80	      next[id] = await calendarIsConnected(id).catch(() => false);
    81	    }
    82	    setConnected(next);
    83	  }
    84	
    85	  useEffect(() => {
    86	    void refreshConnected();
    87	  }, []);
    88	
    89	  async function connectOAuth(provider: 'outlook' | 'google') {
    90	    setBusy(provider);
    91	    setError(null);
    92	    beginOAuth();
    93	    try {
    94	      if (isPersistedLocalOnly()) {
    95	        throw new Error(
    96	          'Local-only mode is on. Turn it off before connecting a calendar, because sign-in contacts the provider.'
    97	        );
    98	      }
    99	      if (provider === 'outlook') await calendarConnectOutlook();
   100	      else await calendarConnectGoogle();
   101	      await refreshConnected();
   102	      void runSync();
   103	    } catch (err) {
   104	      const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
   105	      // The user clicked Cancel — an intentional exit, not a failure.
   106	      if (message !== 'cancelled') setError(message);
   107	      void OAUTH_CANCEL[provider]().catch(() => {});
   108	    } finally {
   109	      setBusy(null);
   110	      endOAuth();
   111	    }
   112	  }
   113	
   114	  // Live cancel while the sign-in browser tab is still open — the
   115	  // catch-block call in connectOAuth only fires after calendarConnectOutlook/
   116	  // Google has already rejected, so it can't abort a pending wait (matches
   117	  // the OneDriveConnect cancelConnect pattern).
   118	  function cancelSignIn(provider: 'outlook' | 'google') {
   119	    OAUTH_CANCEL[provider]().catch(() => {});
   120	  }
   121	
   122	  async function connectIcs() {
   123	    const trimmed = icsUrl.trim();
   124	    if (!trimmed) {
   125	      setError('Paste the calendar’s ICS address first.');
   126	      return;
   127	    }
   128	    // The backend fetches this URL immediately to validate it, so this is a
   129	    // network call — the same local-only guard connectOAuth uses.
   130	    if (isPersistedLocalOnly()) {
   131	      setError(
   132	        'Local-only mode is on. Turn it off before connecting a calendar, because sign-in contacts the provider.'
   133	      );
   134	      return;
   135	    }
   136	    setBusy('ics');
   137	    setError(null);
   138	    try {
   139	      await calendarConnectIcs(trimmed);
   140	      setIcsUrl('');
   141	      await refreshConnected();
   142	      void runSync();
   143	    } catch (err) {
   144	      setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
   145	    } finally {
   146	      setBusy(null);
   147	    }
   148	  }
   149	
   150	  async function runSync() {
   151	    setError(null);
   152	    if (isPersistedLocalOnly()) {
   153	      setError(
   154	        'Local-only mode is on. Turn it off before syncing your calendar, because it contacts the provider.'
   155	      );
   156	      return;
   157	    }
   158	    setSyncing(true);
   159	    try {
   160	      const report = await calendarSyncAll(
   161	        buildCalendarMatterMap(getMatters())
   162	      );
   163	      setLastReport(report);
   164	      setLastSyncedAt(new Date());
   165	      const verb = report.cancelled ? 'stopped after indexing' : 'indexed';
   166	      void calendarAudit
   167	        .logDurable(
   168	          'calendar.sync',

    79	/// Microsoft loopback+PKCE sign-in with Calendars.Read. Mirrors
    80	/// `onedrive_connect` (onedrive/commands.rs:147-203) including the
    81	/// cancel-rollback semantics.
    82	#[tauri::command]
    83	pub async fn calendar_connect_outlook(state: State<'_, CalendarState>) -> Result<(), String> {
    84	    use crate::commands::mail::gmail::oauth::{
    85	        await_redirect_code_or_cancel, bind_loopback_host, gen_pkce, gen_state, open_browser,
    86	        store_or_rollback_on_cancel,
    87	    };
    88	    use super::oauth::{build_ms_auth_url, ms_exchange_code, MS_TOKEN_ENDPOINT};
    89	
    90	    state.oauth_cancel.store(false, Ordering::SeqCst);
    91	    let cancel = state.oauth_cancel.clone();
    92	
    93	    let (verifier, challenge) = gen_pkce();
    94	    let state_token = gen_state();
    95	    // "localhost" host is required for MS personal accounts (BUG-010,
    96	    // documented at mail/gmail/oauth.rs:270-282).
    97	    let (listener, redirect_uri) = bind_loopback_host("localhost")
    98	        .await
    99	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   100	    let url = build_ms_auth_url(&ms_client_id(), &redirect_uri, &challenge, &state_token);
   101	    open_browser(&url);
   102	    let code = await_redirect_code_or_cancel(
   103	        listener,
   104	        &state_token,
   105	        std::time::Duration::from_secs(300),
   106	        cancel.clone(),
   107	    )
   108	    .await
   109	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   110	    let tokens = ms_exchange_code(&ms_client_id(), &code, &verifier, &redirect_uri, MS_TOKEN_ENDPOINT)
   111	        .await
   112	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   113	
   114	    let entry = keyring::Entry::new(&provider_service("outlook")?, KEYCHAIN_REFRESH_KEY)
   115	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   116	    let previous = entry.get_password().ok();
   117	    store_or_rollback_on_cancel(
   118	        &cancel,
   119	        || entry.set_password(&tokens.refresh).map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
   120	        || match &previous {
   121	            Some(prev) => { let _ = entry.set_password(prev); }
   122	            None => { let _ = entry.delete_credential(); }
   123	        },
   124	    )
   125	}

   135	/// Google loopback+PKCE sign-in with calendar.readonly. Mirrors
   136	/// `gmail_connect` (mail/connect.rs) with the calendar auth URL, and — like
   137	/// `calendar_connect_outlook` above — the cancel-rollback semantics from
   138	/// `onedrive_connect` (onedrive/commands.rs:147-203), so closing the sign-in
   139	/// tab actually aborts the wait instead of leaving the card stuck until the
   140	/// 300s timeout (wave-1c review finding, P2).
   141	#[tauri::command]
   142	pub async fn calendar_connect_google(state: State<'_, CalendarState>) -> Result<(), String> {
   143	    use crate::commands::mail::gmail::oauth::{
   144	        await_redirect_code_or_cancel, bind_loopback, gen_pkce, gen_state, open_browser,
   145	        store_or_rollback_on_cancel, GoogleOAuth,
   146	    };
   147	    use crate::commands::mail::{gmail_client_id, gmail_client_secret};
   148	    use super::oauth::build_google_auth_url;
   149	
   150	    state.oauth_cancel.store(false, Ordering::SeqCst);
   151	    let cancel = state.oauth_cancel.clone();
   152	
   153	    let (verifier, challenge) = gen_pkce();
   154	    let state_token = gen_state();
   155	    let (listener, redirect_uri) = bind_loopback().await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   156	    let url = build_google_auth_url(&gmail_client_id(), &redirect_uri, &challenge, &state_token);
   157	    open_browser(&url);
   158	    let code = await_redirect_code_or_cancel(
   159	        listener,
   160	        &state_token,
   161	        std::time::Duration::from_secs(300),
   162	        cancel.clone(),
   163	    )
   164	    .await
   165	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   166	    let oauth = GoogleOAuth::new(gmail_client_id(), gmail_client_secret());
   167	    let tokens = oauth
   168	        .exchange_code(&code, &verifier, &redirect_uri)
   169	        .await
   170	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   171	    let refresh = tokens
   172	        .refresh
   173	        .ok_or("Google did not return a refresh token; try again")?;
   174	    let entry = keyring::Entry::new(&provider_service("google")?, KEYCHAIN_REFRESH_KEY)
   175	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   176	    let previous = entry.get_password().ok();
   177	    store_or_rollback_on_cancel(
   178	        &cancel,
   179	        || entry.set_password(&refresh).map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
   180	        || match &previous {
   181	            Some(prev) => {
   182	                let _ = entry.set_password(prev);
   183	            }
   184	            None => {
   185	                let _ = entry.delete_credential();
   186	            }
   187	        },
   188	    )
   189	}

   191	#[tauri::command]
   192	pub async fn calendar_connect_google_cancel(

   195	    state.oauth_cancel.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);

   198	
   199	/// True for a bare-HTTP URL whose ACTUAL host (per URL syntax, not a naive
   200	/// string split) is loopback (dev/test only). ICS feed URLs commonly embed
   201	/// a secret access token as a query parameter, so plaintext HTTP anywhere
   202	/// else would send that secret over the open network — only localhost is
   203	/// exempt from the HTTPS requirement.
   204	///
   205	/// Wave-1 review finding (P2): a hand-rolled `split(['/', ':', '?'])` reads
   206	/// everything before the first of those chars as "the host", but a URL like
   207	/// `http://localhost:80@evil.example/feed.ics` puts "localhost:80" in the
   208	/// USERINFO component (before '@') — the real host, and where reqwest
   209	/// actually connects, is "evil.example". Parsing with the `url` crate
   210	/// (re-exported as `reqwest::Url`, already used elsewhere in this codebase
   211	/// — see `connector::assert_same_origin`) and reading `host_str()` reflects
   212	/// what the HTTP client will really connect to.
   213	fn is_localhost_http(url: &str) -> bool {
   214	    let Ok(parsed) = reqwest::Url::parse(url) else { return false };
   215	    if parsed.scheme() != "http" {
   216	        return false;
   217	    }
   218	    matches!(parsed.host_str(), Some("localhost") | Some("127.0.0.1") | Some("::1"))
   219	}
   220	
   221	/// ICS fallback: validate the URL shape, fetch it once to prove it parses,
   222	/// then store the URL in the keychain (secret ICS URLs embed a token).
   223	#[tauri::command]
   224	pub async fn calendar_connect_ics(url: String) -> Result<(), String> {
   225	    let trimmed = url.trim().to_string();
   226	    if !trimmed.starts_with("https://") && !is_localhost_http(&trimmed) {
   227	        return Err("Enter the calendar's ICS address (starts with https://).".into());
   228	    }
   229	    let body = super::ics_source::fetch_ics_text(&trimmed)
   230	        .await
   231	        .map_err(|e| format!("Could not read that calendar address: {e}"))?;
   232	    if !body.contains("BEGIN:VCALENDAR") {
   233	        return Err("That address did not return a calendar (ICS) feed.".into());
   234	    }
   235	    keyring::Entry::new(&provider_service("ics")?, KEYCHAIN_ICS_URL_KEY)
   236	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
   237	        .set_password(&trimmed)
   238	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   239	    Ok(())
   240	}
   241	
   242	/// Careful is_connected: NoEntry = false, real keychain error = Err
   243	/// (the mail/mod.rs is_connected pattern, not OneDrive's is_ok()).
   244	#[tauri::command]
   245	pub async fn calendar_is_connected(provider: String) -> Result<bool, String> {
   246	    let entry = keyring::Entry::new(&provider_service(&provider)?, secret_key_for(&provider))
   247	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   248	    match entry.get_password() {
   249	        Ok(_) => Ok(true),
   250	        Err(keyring::Error::NoEntry) => Ok(false),
   251	        Err(e) => Err(e.to_string()),
   252	    }
   253	}
   254	
   255	/// Disconnect ONE provider. When it was the last connected provider, purge
   256	/// the encrypted store, its RAG rows, and the DB master key (the
   257	/// calendly_disconnect ordering: purge RAG chunks -> purge db -> secrets).
   258	#[tauri::command]
   259	pub async fn calendar_disconnect(
   260	    state: State<'_, CalendarState>,
   261	    provider: String,
   262	) -> Result<(), String> {
   263	    let service = provider_service(&provider)?;
   264	    if state
   265	        .is_syncing
   266	        .compare_exchange(false, true, Ordering::SeqCst, Ordering::SeqCst)
   267	        .is_err()
   268	    {
   269	        return Err("A calendar sync is running. Stop it first.".into());
   270	    }
   271	    let result = calendar_disconnect_inner(&state, &provider, &service).await;
   272	    state.is_syncing.store(false, Ordering::SeqCst);
   273	    result
   274	}
   275	
   276	/// Takes `&CalendarState` directly (not `&State<'_, CalendarState>`) —
   277	/// matching the crm_disconnect_logic precedent — so tests can construct a
   278	/// plain `CalendarState` over a temp workspace and call this exact code path
   279	/// without standing up a Tauri app. `State<T>` derefs to `&T`, so the call
   280	/// site above needs no change beyond auto-deref.
   281	pub async fn calendar_disconnect_inner(
   282	    state: &CalendarState,
   283	    provider: &str,
   284	    service: &str,
   285	) -> Result<(), String> {
   286	    let workspace = state.workspace.lock().await.clone();
   287	    // 0. A workspace must be known before we can locate (and purge) any
   288	    //    previously synced data. codex-review P2 (round 5), matching the
   289	    //    established calendly precedent (calendly_disconnect_logic returns
   290	    //    early with `data_remains: true` for exactly this case, never
   291	    //    deleting the credential): silently treating "workspace unset" as
   292	    //    "nothing to purge" and still forgetting the credential would leave
   293	    //    a PRIOR session's synced calendar data on disk (the local DB file
   294	    //    persists across process restarts independently of this in-memory
   295	    //    state) while reporting the provider disconnected.
   296	    let Some(ws) = workspace.as_ref() else {
   297	        return Err(
   298	            "No workspace is open, so this connection's data could not be located. \
   299	             Open the workspace and try disconnecting again."
   300	                .to_string(),
   301	        );
   302	    };
   303	    // 1. Purge this provider's RAG rows, then its store rows + cursors. Row
   304	    //    deletion runs even when other providers remain connected —
   305	    //    codex-review P2: without it, a disconnected provider's events
   306	    //    silently persisted in the shared encrypted store and could
   307	    //    resurface via list_in_window / a later indexing pass.
   308	    //
   309	    //    The local store purge (open + delete_provider_rows) MUST succeed
   310	    //    before step 2 forgets the credential — codex-review P2 (round 2):
   311	    //    forgetting the credential while the purge silently failed would
   312	    //    report "disconnected" while private calendar data is still on
   313	    //    disk, with the UI now showing nothing wrong.
   314	    //
   315	    //    RAG chunk deletion is likewise now REQUIRED, not best-effort
   316	    //    (polish-1 item 3, 2026-07-03): a swallowed `let _ = ...` here used
   317	    //    to let a LanceDB outage or a master-key read failure silently
   318	    //    leave this provider's synced content sitting in the vector store
   319	    //    forever with no retry path, while the disconnect still reported
   320	    //    success and deleted the very credential that would let a future
   321	    //    reconnect re-identify and re-purge that content. Every step below
   322	    //    now aborts the WHOLE disconnect on failure, before the credential
   323	    //    or local rows are touched, so a retry re-attempts the identical
   324	    //    purge from an unchanged, consistent state — the same "keep state
   325	    //    consistent, let the user retry" answer the calendly/CRM connector
   326	    //    disconnects use (there via a `data_remains` result flag; here via
   327	    //    Err, since this command's signature is Result<(), String>).
   328	    {
   329	        let store = CalendarStore::open(ws).map_err(|e| {
   330	            format!("Could not open the calendar store to remove this connection's data: {e}")

exec

     1	//! polish-1 item 3: calendar_disconnect must not report success (nor delete
     2	//! the local rows or credential) when the RAG/vector-store purge fails.
     3	//! True end-to-end tests against a real temp workspace + real LanceDB
     4	//! dataset dir, mirroring the crm_purge_e2e_* precedent in
     5	//! crm_fixture_import.rs — but for the FAILURE path, which had no coverage
     6	//! anywhere in this codebase before this ticket.
     7	
     8	use lantern_lib::commands::calendar::commands::{calendar_disconnect_inner, CalendarState};
     9	use lantern_lib::commands::calendar::model::{CalendarEvent, CalendarProvider};
    10	use lantern_lib::commands::calendar::store::CalendarStore;
    11	use std::path::Path;
    12	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicU32};
    13	use std::sync::Arc;
    14	
    15	fn test_state(ws: std::path::PathBuf) -> CalendarState {
    16	    CalendarState {
    17	        workspace: tokio::sync::Mutex::new(Some(ws)),
    18	        is_syncing: Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false)),
    19	        cancel: Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false)),
    20	        oauth_cancel: Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false)),
    21	        last_report: tokio::sync::Mutex::new(None),
    22	        progress_events: Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0)),
    23	    }
    24	}
    25	
    26	/// Headless-test override for the calendar SQLCipher master key (see
    27	/// calendar/store.rs's `calendar_master_key`) — `CalendarStore::open` is the
    28	/// first thing `calendar_disconnect_inner` does, and without this override
    29	/// it hits a real OS keychain read that blocks indefinitely in a sandbox
    30	/// with no Secret Service/D-Bus backend, rather than failing fast.
    31	fn calendar_test_key_hex() -> String {
    32	    "33".repeat(32)
    33	}
    34	
    35	/// Both tests below mutate the same two process-global env vars; Rust's
    36	/// default test runner executes tests in this binary on separate threads
    37	/// concurrently, so without serializing them one test's env var could leak
    38	/// into the other mid-run. Held for each test's full body.
    39	static ENV_GUARD: std::sync::Mutex<()> = std::sync::Mutex::new(());
    40	
    41	/// Seed one Outlook event, indexed under one matter, so
    42	/// `list_indexed_rag_source_ids()` returns exactly one row scoped to the
    43	/// "outlook" provider prefix `calendar_disconnect_inner` filters on.
    44	fn seed_indexed_event(ws: &Path, event_id: &str, matter: &str) {
    45	    let store = CalendarStore::open(ws).expect("open calendar store");
    46	    let event = CalendarEvent {
    47	        id: event_id.to_string(),
    48	        provider: CalendarProvider::Outlook,
    49	        title: "Henderson check-in".to_string(),
    50	        description: String::new(),
    51	        start_utc: "2026-07-02T16:00:00Z".to_string(),
    52	        end_utc: "2026-07-02T17:00:00Z".to_string(),
    53	        attendees: vec![],
    54	        organizer_email: "adv@firm.com".to_string(),
    55	        is_cancelled: false,
    56	        self_declined: false,
    57	    };
    58	    store.upsert_event(&event, "hash1").expect("upsert event");
    59	    store
    60	        .mark_indexed(event_id, "hash1", matter)
    61	        .expect("mark indexed");
    62	}
    63	
    64	/// NOTE (sandbox limitation): the disconnect's LATER steps — deleting the
    65	/// provider's own credential, and reading the other two providers' keychain
    66	/// entries to decide whether to purge the whole store — hit the real OS
    67	/// keychain with no headless-test override, which blocks indefinitely in a
    68	/// sandbox with no Secret Service/D-Bus backend rather than failing fast.
    69	/// Both tests below are constructed so `calendar_disconnect_inner` returns
    70	/// (with an Err) INSIDE the purge block, before any of those later steps —
    71	/// exactly the ordering this fix establishes — so they never reach that
    72	/// untestable-here code. A "purge succeeds, so the rest of the disconnect
    73	/// runs" test is not included for the same reason: it cannot complete in
    74	/// this environment without hanging.
    75	#[tokio::test]
    76	async fn purge_failure_from_a_bad_master_key_leaves_local_rows_and_index_untouched() {
    77	    let _guard = ENV_GUARD.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    78	    std::env::set_var(
    79	        "KEEPANCE_HEADLESS_TEST_CALENDAR_MASTER_KEY_HEX",
    80	        &calendar_test_key_hex(),
    81	    );
    82	    let workspace = tempfile::tempdir().expect("workspace tempdir");
    83	    let ws = workspace.path().to_path_buf();
    84	    seed_indexed_event(&ws, "outlook:e1", "matter-1");
    85	
    86	    // Force rag::crypto::get_or_create_master_key() to fail deterministically:
    87	    // its headless-test override requires exactly 32 bytes of hex.
    88	    std::env::set_var("KEEPANCE_HEADLESS_TEST_VECTORS_MASTER_KEY_HEX", "00");
    89	    let state = test_state(ws.clone());
    90	    let result = calendar_disconnect_inner(&state, "outlook", "keepance-calendar-ms-test").await;
    91	    std::env::remove_var("KEEPANCE_HEADLESS_TEST_VECTORS_MASTER_KEY_HEX");
    92	
    93	    assert!(
    94	        result.is_err(),
    95	        "a master-key read failure must abort the disconnect, not report success"
    96	    );
    97	    let store = CalendarStore::open(&ws).expect("reopen calendar store");
    98	    let remaining = store
    99	        .list_indexed_rag_source_ids()
   100	        .expect("list indexed rag source ids");
   101	    std::env::remove_var("KEEPANCE_HEADLESS_TEST_CALENDAR_MASTER_KEY_HEX");
   102	    assert_eq!(
   103	        remaining,
   104	        vec!["calendar:outlook:e1:matter-1".to_string()],
   105	        "the RAG source id must still be listed as indexed — nothing was purged"
   106	    );
   107	}
   108	
   109	#[tokio::test]
   110	async fn purge_failure_from_an_unavailable_vector_store_leaves_local_rows_untouched() {
   111	    let _guard = ENV_GUARD.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
   112	    std::env::set_var(
   113	        "KEEPANCE_HEADLESS_TEST_CALENDAR_MASTER_KEY_HEX",

   121	    // FILE at the exact path open_connection's create_dir_all needs, the

   123	    let dataset_path = lantern_lib::commands::rag::store::dataset_path(&ws);

   130	    let result = calendar_disconnect_inner(&state, "outlook", "keepance-calendar-ms-test").await;

codex
The calendar smoke has a procedure clue: the AI worker cancelled the app’s in-progress sign-in before Jameson completed the passkey step. The backend is written to roll back saved tokens when that cancel flag is set. So completing the browser auth after the app-side cancel would not count as a connected calendar.

   413	/// Fresh MS access token from the stored refresh token (rotation-aware;
   414	/// the onedrive/commands.rs shape).
   415	pub(crate) async fn fresh_ms_access_token() -> Result<String, String> {
   416	    let entry = keyring::Entry::new(
   417	        &crate::identity::calendar_keychain_service("ms"),
   418	        KEYCHAIN_REFRESH_KEY,
   419	    )
   420	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   421	    let rt = entry.get_password().map_err(|_| "not connected".to_string())?;
   422	    let auth = super::oauth::OAuth::new(ms_client_id());
   423	    match auth.refresh(&rt).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
   424	        super::oauth::TokenOutcome::Tokens { access, refresh, .. } => {
   425	            if let Some(new_rt) = refresh {
   426	                if let Err(e) = entry.set_password(&new_rt) {
   427	                    log::warn!("calendar MS refresh-token rotation not saved: {e}");
   428	                }
   429	            }
   430	            Ok(access)
   431	        }
   432	        super::oauth::TokenOutcome::Failed(e) if e == "invalid_grant" || e == "invalid_scope" => {
   433	            Err("scope_upgrade_required".to_string())
   434	        }
   435	        super::oauth::TokenOutcome::Failed(e) => Err(format!("refresh failed: {e}")),
   436	        _ => Err("unexpected refresh outcome".into()),
   437	    }
   438	}
   439	
   440	/// Fresh Google access token (the mail gmail refresh shape).
   441	pub(crate) async fn fresh_google_access_token() -> Result<String, String> {
   442	    use crate::commands::mail::{gmail_client_id, gmail_client_secret};
   443	    let entry = keyring::Entry::new(
   444	        &crate::identity::calendar_keychain_service("google"),
   445	        KEYCHAIN_REFRESH_KEY,
   446	    )
   447	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   448	    let rt = entry.get_password().map_err(|_| "not connected".to_string())?;
   449	    let oauth = crate::commands::mail::gmail::oauth::GoogleOAuth::new(
   450	        gmail_client_id(),
   451	        gmail_client_secret(),
   452	    );
   453	    match oauth.refresh(&rt).await {
   454	        Ok(tokens) => Ok(tokens.access),
   455	        Err(e) => {
   456	            let msg = e.to_string();
   457	            if msg.contains("invalid_grant") || msg.contains("invalid_scope") {
   458	                Err("scope_upgrade_required".to_string())
   459	            } else {
   460	                Err(msg)
   461	            }
   462	        }
   463	    }
   464	}

   260	// ── Loopback listener ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   261	
   262	/// Bind a loopback TCP listener on an OS-assigned port.
   263	/// Returns `(listener, redirect_uri)` where `redirect_uri` is
   264	/// `"http://127.0.0.1:<port>"` — suitable to pass to Google and to
   265	/// `exchange_code`.
   266	pub async fn bind_loopback() -> anyhow::Result<(tokio::net::TcpListener, String)> {
   267	    bind_loopback_host("127.0.0.1").await
   268	}
   269	
   270	/// Bind an ephemeral loopback listener for an OAuth redirect on a SPECIFIC host
   271	/// ("127.0.0.1" or "localhost"), returning the listener and the matching
   272	/// `http://<host>:<port>` redirect URI.
   273	///
   274	/// Why the host matters (BUG-010): the redirect URI host and the bound listener
   275	/// host must be the SAME name, because the browser resolves the redirect host
   276	/// and connects there. On Windows, "localhost" resolves to ::1 (IPv6) first —
   277	/// so binding 127.0.0.1 (IPv4) while redirecting to "localhost" left the browser
   278	/// hitting a dead ::1 port ("localhost refused to connect") and the listener
   279	/// never received the code. Binding the literal "localhost" makes tokio resolve
   280	/// it the same way the browser will, so they always meet. Microsoft personal
   281	/// accounts require the "localhost" redirect (they reject a numeric 127.0.0.1
   282	/// loopback); Gmail keeps 127.0.0.1.
   283	pub async fn bind_loopback_host(host: &str) -> anyhow::Result<(tokio::net::TcpListener, String)> {
   284	    let listener = tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(format!("{host}:0")).await?;
   285	    let port = listener.local_addr()?.port();
   286	    let redirect_uri = format!("http://{host}:{port}");
   287	    Ok((listener, redirect_uri))
   288	}
   289	
   290	/// Open the system browser to `auth_url`.
   291	/// Best-effort: logs a warning on failure but does not return an error
   292	/// (the user can also copy-paste the URL).
   293	pub fn open_browser(auth_url: &str) {
   294	    // Routed through the shared no-window opener so Windows does not flash a
   295	    // console window when launching the browser for the Gmail OAuth consent.
   296	    crate::util::proc::open_url(auth_url);
   297	}
   298	
   299	/// Accept ONE connection on `listener`, read the GET request line,
   300	/// verify `state`, write a "you can close this tab" response, and return
   301	/// the authorization code. Times out after `timeout`.
   302	///
   303	/// This function drains the rest of the browser's HTTP request headers before
   304	/// closing so the OS sends a clean TCP FIN instead of a RST. Without draining,
   305	/// closing a socket with unread data causes the OS to send a RST, which makes
   306	/// the browser show `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` and a blank page.
   307	pub async fn await_redirect_code(
   308	    listener: tokio::net::TcpListener,
   309	    expected_state: &str,
   310	    timeout: Duration,
   311	) -> anyhow::Result<String> {
   312	    let expected_state = expected_state.to_string();
   313	
   314	    tokio::time::timeout(timeout, async move {
   315	        let (mut socket, _peer) = listener.accept().await?;
   316	
   317	        // Read bytes until we see \r\n (end of request line) or the buffer fills.
   318	        let mut buf = Vec::with_capacity(4096);
   319	        let mut tmp = [0u8; 1];
   320	        loop {
   321	            socket.read_exact(&mut tmp).await?;
   322	            buf.push(tmp[0]);
   323	            if buf.ends_with(b"\r\n") {
   324	                break;
   325	            }
   326	            if buf.len() > 8192 {
   327	                anyhow::bail!("request line too long");
   328	            }
   329	        }

 succeeded in 0ms:
   429	pub fn store_or_rollback_on_cancel(
   430	    cancel: &std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool,
   431	    store: impl FnOnce() -> Result<(), String>,
   432	    rollback: impl FnOnce(),
   433	) -> Result<(), String> {
   434	    use std::sync::atomic::Ordering;
   435	    if cancel.load(Ordering::SeqCst) {
   436	        return Err("cancelled".to_string());
   437	    }
   438	    store()?;
   439	    if cancel.load(Ordering::SeqCst) {
   440	        rollback();
   441	        return Err("cancelled".to_string());
   442	    }
   443	    Ok(())
   444	}
   445	
   446	/// Read and discard bytes from `socket` until the end-of-headers marker
   447	/// `\r\n\r\n` is seen or 16 KB have been consumed, whichever comes first.
   448	/// Best-effort: ignores errors (the browser may have already closed its send half).
   449	async fn drain_request_headers(socket: &mut tokio::net::TcpStream) {
   450	    const CAP: usize = 16 * 1024;
   451	    let mut drained = Vec::with_capacity(512);
   452	    let mut tmp = [0u8; 1];
   453	    while drained.len() < CAP {
   454	        match socket.read_exact(&mut tmp).await {
   455	            Ok(_) => {
   456	                drained.push(tmp[0]);
   457	                if drained.ends_with(b"\r\n\r\n") {
   458	                    break;
   459	                }
   460	            }
   461	            Err(_) => break, // EOF or connection error — nothing more to drain
   462	        }

codex
The calendar “Today’s meetings” strip does exist in the shell, but it only has something to show after `calendarListEvents` can read stored events. If the app never stores the calendar token, sync never runs, so the strip has no source data.

    39	/**
    40	 * Plausible client-identifying addresses for an unmatched event, deduped.
    41	 * COORDINATOR FINDING (P1, merge blocker): organizerEmail and the first
    42	 * attendee's email can be the SAME person's two roles (advisor organizes,
    43	 * client attends) OR two DIFFERENT people (client organizes, advisor is
    44	 * the only attendee — resolveMattersForCalendarEvent already handles this
    45	 * asymmetry for matching). When they differ, we genuinely cannot tell which
    46	 * one is the client without asking — teaching the wrong one would silently
    47	 * misfile every future meeting carrying that address. A single candidate
    48	 * (the common case: they're equal, or only one is present) stays teachable
    49	 * with no extra step.

    51	function candidateKeysForEvent(event: CalendarEventDto): string[] {
    52	  const raw = [event.organizerEmail, event.attendees[0]?.email].filter(
    53	    (k): k is string => Boolean(k)
    54	  );
    55	  return Array.from(new Set(raw));
    56	}

     1	/**
     2	 * MatterNotesEditor — live collaborative notes for a shared matter.
     3	 *
     4	 * Binds a CodeMirror 6 editor to `doc.getText('notes')` on a MatterSyncClient's
     5	 * Yjs document via y-codemirror.next's `yCollab` extension. Two clients sharing
     6	 * the same matter key will see each other's edits converge in real time.
     7	 *
     8	 * Fail-closed: when `syncClient` is null (key unavailable, walled, or not yet
     9	 * published) the editor is replaced with a friendly message and no editor
    10	 * mounts — nothing to leak.
    11	 *
    12	 * Disk mirror: on meaningful local change we debounce-write the notes text to
    13	 * `<first folderPath of the matter>/matter-notes.md` via WorkspaceService. This
    14	 * is a READ-ONLY artifact of the synced truth — solo tooling and search still
    15	 * see it. Never write to it directly; the Yjs doc is the source of truth.
    16	 *
    17	 * Presence (remote cursors): y-codemirror.next exposes cursor awareness via
    18	 * `yRemoteSelections` when an `awareness` object is passed to `yCollab`. The
    19	 * MatterSyncClient does NOT expose an awareness channel on its public API (the
    20	 * relay is a dumb pipe for opaque update blobs only — there is no separate
    21	 * awareness broadcast channel). Therefore presence is SKIPPED in this release.
    22	 * Adding it would require either: (a) an awareness channel on the relay (new
    23	 * backend endpoint), or (b) a side-channel in the Yjs doc itself. Neither is
    24	 * in scope for Task 4. Cursor awareness is the named next increment.
    25	 */
    26	
    27	import { useEffect, useRef, useCallback, useState } from 'react';
    28	import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';
    29	import { EditorState } from '@codemirror/state';
    30	import { EditorView, keymap, drawSelection } from '@codemirror/view';
    31	import { defaultKeymap, history, historyKeymap } from '@codemirror/commands';
    32	import { syntaxHighlighting, defaultHighlightStyle } from '@codemirror/language';
    33	import { markdown, markdownLanguage } from '@codemirror/lang-markdown';

    42	/** How long the "added" confirmation stays visible after Send to Wealthbox. */
